<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a uniform classical randomized decision algorithm for worst-case Subset Sum. Under every fixed polynomial bound on input-integer bit length, it uses $`\mathop{\mathrm{poly}}\nolimits (n)2^{n/2}`$ time and ordinary $`O(2^{n/5})`$ writable words of $`O(n+b)`$ bits, where <i>b</i> is the largest input bit length. Both resource bounds hold on every execution. The error is one-sided: the algorithm always rejects unsolvable instances and accepts each solvable instance with probability at least 2/3.

- **claim-uniform-subset-sum** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a single uniform classical randomized algorithm in this model that never answers YES incorrectly and answers each YES instance correctly with probability at least $2/3$. For every fixed positive integer $c$, there are a constant $A_c>0$ and positive integers $k_c,N_c$ such that, whenever $n\ge N_c$ and $b\le n^c$, every outcome of the algorithm’s randomness uses at most $$A_c n^{k_c}2^{n/2}\quad\text{operations} \qquad\text{and}\qquad A_c(n+b+2)^{15}2^{0.199n}\quad\text{writable words}.$$ The algorithm does not depend on $c$.
  - Explanation: A uniform randomized decision algorithm combines sound acceptance with simultaneous time and workspace bounds on every execution. Correctness holds individually, while the asymptotic resource guarantee is scoped to each fixed polynomial-bit class.
- **claim-ordinary-space-absorption** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-target)): For every fixed $\rho>0.199$, the same algorithm has ordinary $O_{c,\rho}(2^{\rho n})$ word space under each fixed polynomial-bit convention, with the time and correctness guarantees of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. In particular, it has $O_c(2^{n/5})$ word space.
  - Explanation: A fixed increase in the space exponent absorbs the polynomial workspace factor. This supplies the ordinary space guarantee while retaining the same time and correctness guarantees.
- **claim-deficiency-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-deficiency)): For arbitrary fixed weights on the $n$ indices, deficiency is nonnegative, invariant under sign changes of individual weights, inclusion-monotone, and superadditive on disjoint domains. Replacing one index in a domain of fixed size changes its deficiency by at most one. If $V$ is uniform among the size-$v$ domains, $0\le v\le n$, then $$\label{eq:deficiency-variance} \operatorname{Var}(\mathrm{d}(V))\le v.$$
  - Explanation: Deficiency measures collisions among subset sums and behaves monotonically under domain growth. Its replacement sensitivity bounds the variance for a uniformly sampled domain without any random-input assumption.
- **claim-binomial-entropy-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy)): For integers $0\le k\le v$, $$\frac{2^{v h(k/v)}}{v+1}\le \binom vk\le 2^{v h(k/v)}.$$ For $v=k=0$, interpret $v h(k/v)$ as zero. Entropy is concave, symmetric about $1/2$, and increasing on $[0,1/2]$. Moreover, $$\label{eq:entropy-numbers} h_*<.8114,\qquad h(1/3)<14/15,\qquad h(1/6)>5/8.$$
  - Explanation: Binary entropy bounds fixed-cardinality subset counts with explicit polynomial loss. The strict numerical inequalities are established using exact integer comparisons rather than floating-point optimization.
- **claim-prime-collision-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prime)): Let $4\le\ell\le n$ be an integer. The set $$\mathcal P_\ell= \{p\text{ prime}:p<2^\ell,\ 4\ell p\ge2^\ell\}$$ has at least $2^\ell/(4\ell)$ elements. If $p$ is uniform on this set and $\Delta$ is a fixed nonzero integer with $\|\Delta\|<2^{2u}$, then $$\Pr[p\mid\Delta]\le 8u^2 2^{-\ell}.$$ In particular, this estimate applies to every nonzero actual-weight difference used by the two trials.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently large finite prime range supports uniform sampling and bounds divisibility of a fixed unequal-weight difference. The bound retains the dependence on input bit length.
- **claim-filtered-streams-tie-replay** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-streams)): Let $A_1,A_2$ be stored constant-word item arrays whose indices have $O(n)$ bits. Let $p\mid P$ be positive integers, and let $c,v$ be specified integer residues. Assume that the data occupy constantly many words each and that arithmetic, comparisons, and indexed access on them take $O(1)$ word operations in the stated model. The pairs $(a,b)\in A_1\times A_2$ whose actual weights sum to $c\pmod p$ can be streamed in nondecreasing order of either $$(\omega(a)+\omega(b))\bmod P \quad\text{or}\quad (v-\omega(a)-\omega(b))\bmod P,$$ where all residues are normalized to $\{0,\ldots,P-1\}$. Initialization costs $O(n(\|A_1\|+\|A_2\|+1))$ word operations, each subsequent advance costs $O(n)$, and the state together with the input arrays uses $O(\|A_1\|+\|A_2\|+1)$ words.* *Consider two such streams of lengths $N_1,N_2$, with combined states and arrays of at most $S$ words. If their equal-key Cartesian products contain $Z$ pairs in all, they can report all these pairs using $O(S+B)$ words and, apart from initialization and the work performed on reported pairs, $$\label{eq:tie-cost} O\left(B+n(N_1+N_2+Z)+\frac SB Z\right)$$ operations. Here $B\ge1$ is an integer buffer capacity. In particular, if $S\le\operatorname{poly}(n)B$, the bound is $\operatorname{poly}(n)(B+N_1+N_2+Z)$. The same upper bounds hold for an execution stopped partway, with $Z$ still counting *all potential* matches. No coprimality assumption is needed.
  - Explanation: Modularly eligible pair occurrences can be streamed in sorted order from stored arrays. Buffered tie joins preserve multiplicity and charge replay to potential matching pairs, including executions stopped before completion.
- **claim-compressed-block-trial** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-compressed)): For sufficiently large main-branch inputs, the compressed-block trial uses at most $u^{15}2^{n/2}$ word operations on every random outcome, including bounded sampling, and uses $O(u(C+1)+B+2^s+2^{.14n})$ writable words. Every returned YES is correct. If the input is a YES instance and $$\label{alg:high-deficiency} \mu\ge .00765n,$$ its success probability is bounded below by an absolute positive constant.
  - Explanation: The compressed trial keeps distinct weights on disjoint domains, streams combinations, and skips oversized residue dictionaries. Its resource bounds hold without favorable randomness; a high mean deficiency supplies a positive success probability.
- **claim-parent-deduplication-soundness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-validity)): Every retained parent entry has a subset witness on its parent’s indices. Deduplicating entries by actual weight and top mask preserves the existence of every valid final match. Every YES returned by either trial is correct.
  - Explanation: Local disjointness gives each retained parent record a subset witness. Keeping the exact weight together with the top mask preserves compatibility across parents, and the sign-toggle identity converts a signed match to an original solution.
- **claim-prefix-accounting** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prefix)): Fix positive integers $C,q$ and a nonnegative integer $m$. On each side $j\in\{L,R\}$, let $\mathcal T_j$ be a finite collection of raw tuple occurrences. Each occurrence has a bucket in $\{0,\ldots,q-1\}$ and a top mask in $\{0,1\}^m$. A valid occurrence produces an entry consisting of its exact weight and top mask. Assume that occurrences producing the same entry have the same bucket. Let $\mathcal U_j$ be a finite ambient set containing all distinct valid entries, with bucket and mask labels extending those of the generated entries. Write $$J_j=\|\mathcal T_j\|,\qquad U_j=\|\mathcal U_j\|.$$ A task for bucket $z$ and prefix $\sigma$ generates exactly the raw occurrences with these labels, unless it stops early, and reports overflow at the $(C+1)$st distinct valid entry. Suppose a task with $Z$ potential occurrences costs at most $H+KZ$, even if stopped partway, for fixed nonnegative $H,K$.* *Apply the left-skip and right-prefix schedule of Section <a href="#alg:parents" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="alg:parents">4.3</a> to these data, ignoring early YES answers and the operation cap. Then its number $T$ of parent-generation tasks, its total parent-generation work $W_{\mathrm{parent}}$, and the total input-array length $N_{\mathrm{check}}$ of its final checks satisfy $$\begin{aligned} T&\le 2q+2(m+1)U_R/C,\label{eq:prefix-tasks}\\ W_{\mathrm{parent}} &\le H\bigl(2q+2(m+1)U_R/C\bigr) +K\bigl(J_L+(m+1)J_R\bigr),\label{eq:prefix-work}\\ N_{\mathrm{check}}&\le U_L+(2m+3)U_R.\label{eq:prefix-checks} \end{aligned}$$ Every reuse of a left array counts separately in $N_{\mathrm{check}}$.
  - Explanation: Prefix refinement separates task starts, raw tuple processing, and final-check input lengths. Distinct valid entries bound overflow branching, while raw occurrences are charged by prefix depth even when later rejected for overlap.
- **claim-weighted-disjointness** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-separator)): For the input and independent families just specified, there is a weighted-disjointness check with the following guarantees, for all sufficiently large $n$.* 1. *It returns YES only for a pair satisfying <a href="#sep:target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sep:target">[sep:target]</a>. For every fixed pair satisfying that relation, the probability that the check finds a solution is at least $1-4e^{-10}$.* 2. *Preprocessing the families takes at most $\operatorname{poly}(n)2^{2g}$ operations and words. Thereafter the expected check work is at most $$(\|\mathcal L\|+\|\mathcal R\|)\,u^{22}2^{(1-h_*)m}.$$* 3. *Workspace, including preprocessing, is deterministically at most $O(nC+n^2+2^{2g})$ words. Any execution stopped early has no larger work or space usage than the complete traversal used in these bounds.* *The probability statements concern the stated ideal uniform families. Generating them with at most $n^3$ attempts per ordinary rejection draw also has deterministic cost $\operatorname{poly}(n)2^{2g}$, or reports sampling failure. It can be coupled to the ideal families with failure probability $\exp(-\Omega(n^2))$.
  - Explanation: Independent biased separating families reduce mask compatibility to exact complementary-weight searches. Coverage is for a fixed solution pair, workspace is deterministic, and expected traversal work includes an exponential incidence factor.
- **claim-separator-family-reuse** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-separator-reuse)): Fix a finite list of checks satisfying the input conditions of Lemma <a href="#lem:separator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:separator">10</a>, with common block parameters, and let $$N_{\mathrm{check}}=\sum_{\text{checks}}(\|\mathcal L\|+\|\mathcal R\|).$$ The list may itself be random, provided that, conditional on the list and its parameters, the four families have the independent distributions <a href="#sep:families" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sep:families">[sep:families]</a>. In the ideal experiment, the expected total check work conditional on the list and its parameters is at most $$W_0=N_{\mathrm{check}} u^{22}2^{(1-h_*)m}.$$ Preprocessing is paid once. Processing checks one at a time uses the check workspace of Lemma <a href="#lem:separator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:separator">10</a>, in addition to any storage retained by the caller. For any $0<\delta<1$, total check work exceeds $W_0/\delta$ with probability at most $\delta$. Suppose a designated check and a solution pair in it are also chosen, and that conditional on the list, its parameters, and these choices, the families still have the independent distributions <a href="#sep:families" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sep:families">[sep:families]</a>. Then with probability at least $1-4e^{-10}-\delta$ the pair is covered and the total check work is at most $W_0/\delta$.
  - Explanation: The same families can serve a finite list of checks when their conditional product law survives fixing the list and designated witness. Linearity of expectation and a union bound control aggregate work and coverage without independence between those events.
- **claim-low-filter-representation-survival** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-representation)): For a main-branch YES input with $\mu<.00765n$, ideal sampling produces, with probability at least $u^{-100}$, a passing spread test and a representation of a solution satisfying all prescribed leaf counts and all low modular filters. Its two parent weights $x_L,x_R$, with $x_L+x_R=t'$, and all of its masks can be fixed as functions of the nonfamily choices preceding $q$. Conditional on these choices, $q$ still has its prescribed independent prime law; the separating families also retain their prescribed product law, with the now fixed layout parameters.
  - Explanation: In the low-mean YES regime, balanced signed solution pieces retain sufficiently many distinct sums to hit the low modular filters. A surviving witness is selected for analysis before the extra prime and separating families, preserving their required conditional laws.
- **claim-bounded-representation-trial-success** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-resources)): On a YES input with $\mu<.00765n$, a representation trial with the bounded samplers and operation cap succeeds with probability at least $u^{-110}$, for sufficiently large main-branch inputs.
  - Explanation: Unconditional resource-failure bounds are subtracted from rare representation survival before the extra prime and separating families are exposed. The remaining favorable executions complete under the operation cap despite bounded sampling.
- **claim-bounded-uniform-samplers** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-samplers)): A uniform integer in $\{0,\ldots,D-1\}$, with $1\le D<2^w$, can be sampled using at most $n^3$ rejection attempts, or the trial can report failure. Conditional on any preceding history and on this call accepting, its result is uniform in the specified range. It couples to an ideal draw with failure probability at most $2^{-n^3}$.* *For $4\le\ell\le n$, the same attempt limit gives a uniform prime in $\mathcal P_\ell$, conditional on this call accepting, and coupling failure at most $e^{-n^2/4}$. A full trial, including permutations, signs, residues and the four separating families, couples to the ideal experiment with total failure $\exp(-\Omega(n^2))$. All constants are absolute. All permitted attempts are included in the deterministic work bounds.
  - Explanation: Bounded rejection sampling returns a uniform value conditional on call acceptance and otherwise fails the trial. Coupling to ideal sampling accounts for adaptive histories without conditioning the entire execution on successful draws.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Subset Sum** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Given positive integers $a_1,\ldots,a_n$ and a nonnegative target $t$, *Subset Sum* asks whether there is a set of indices $I\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}$ such that $\sum_{i\in I}a_i=t$.
- **Input count** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The parameter $n\ge2$ counts the input integers, not their total encoding length.
- **Word-model size parameters** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Put $$\begin{gathered} b=\max\left(1,\left\lceil \log_2\bigl(1+\max(t,a_1,\ldots,a_n)\bigr)\right\rceil\right), \qquad u=n+b+2,\\ w=\left\lceil4\bigl(n+b+\log_2(n+2)\bigr)\right\rceil. \end{gathered}$$
- **Working space** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Space is the maximum number of writable words simultaneously in use.
- **Polynomial-bit convention** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Our polynomial-bit convention fixes a positive integer $c$ and considers inputs with $b\le n^c$.
- **Deficiency** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For a domain $V\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}$, let $N(V)$ count the *distinct integer values* of its subset sums, and define $$\mathrm{d}(V)=\|V\|-\log N(V).$$
- **Binary entropy** ([source](#eq:parameters)): We use the parameters $$\label{eq:parameters} \begin{gathered} u=n+b+2,\qquad m=8\lfloor3n/100\rfloor,\qquad s=m/2,\qquad g=m/4,\\ C=2^{\lfloor .199n\rfloor},\qquad B=2^{\lfloor .19n\rfloor},\qquad h_*=h(1/4), \end{gathered}$$ where $h(x)=-x\log x-(1-x)\log(1-x)$ has its continuous endpoint values.
- **Dictionary and stream capacities** ([source](#eq:parameters)): The capacity $C$ counts distinct dictionary entries, whereas $B$ counts buffered occurrences in a stream.
- **Main branch** ([source](#eq:guard)): The constructions below concern the *main branch*, defined by $n\ge n_0$ and $$\label{eq:guard} u\le 2^{\lfloor n/10^9\rfloor},$$ for one sufficiently large absolute integer $n_0$ fixed in the program.
- **Actual item weight** ([source](#sec:streams)): Write $\omega(a)$ for the actual integer weight of an item $a$.
- **Parent dictionary record** ([source](#alg:parents)): A parent dictionary stores records $(x,D)$: the exact signed integer weight $x$ and the indexed top mask $D\subseteq M_0$.
- **Parent task** ([source](#alg:parents)): A parent task consists of a side, a target residue modulo $P$, and a possibly empty prefix of the $M_0$ mask in a fixed index order.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-yes-acceptance | Acceptance probability on a YES instance | ≥ | $2/3$ | Every individual YES input. |
| q-main-operation-bound | Word operations on every random outcome | ≤ | $A_c n^{k_c}2^{n/2}$ | Constants and threshold may depend on the fixed exponent; the program does not. |
| q-main-writable-space | Simultaneously writable words on every random outcome | ≤ | $A_c(n+b+2)^{15}2^{0.199n}$ | Each fixed polynomial-bit class. |
| q-ordinary-space-threshold | Admissible ordinary-space exponent | > | $0.199$ | A fixed exponent strictly above the endpoint. |
| q-dictionary-capacity | Distinct-entry dictionary capacity | = | $2^{\lfloor .199n\rfloor}$ | Input integer count. |
| q-stream-buffer-capacity | Buffered occurrence capacity | = | $2^{\lfloor .19n\rfloor}$ | Input integer count. |
| q-domain-deficiency-variance | Variance of deficiency of a random fixed-size domain | ≤ | $v$ | Every feasible domain size. |
| q-quarter-entropy-bound | Entropy at the quarter-cardinality ratio | < | $.8114$ | The fixed entropy constant. |
| q-prime-range-size | Number of primes in the specified finite range | ≥ | $2^\ell/(4\ell)$ | Integer prime-range parameter. |
| q-prime-divisibility-probability | Probability that the sampled prime divides a fixed nonzero difference | ≤ | $8u^2 2^{-\ell}$ | The difference is fixed independently of the prime. |
| q-tie-join-work | Tie-join operations apart from initialization and reported-pair processing | ≤ | $O\left(B+n(N_1+N_2+Z)+\frac SB Z\right)$ | Potential matches include those not reached in a stopped execution. |
| q-compressed-trial-work | Compressed-block trial word operations | ≤ | $u^{15}2^{n/2}$ | Main branch. |
| q-prefix-task-count | Number of parent-generation tasks | ≤ | $2q+2(m+1)U_R/C$ | Complete hypothetical schedule ignoring early YES and the operation cap. |
| q-prefix-check-inputs | Total final-check input-array length | ≤ | $U_L+(2m+3)U_R$ | Counts occurrence lengths across all final checks. |
| q-fixed-pair-separator-coverage | Probability of finding a fixed valid weighted-disjointness pair | ≥ | $1-4e^{-10}$ | A fixed pair independent of ideal uniform separating families. |
| q-separator-expected-work | Expected weighted-disjointness check work after preprocessing | ≤ | $Thereafter the expected check work is at most $$(\|\mathcal L\|+\|\mathcal R\|)\,u^{22}2^{(1-h_*)m}.$$$ | Expectation over independent ideal separating families. |
| q-separator-workspace | Weighted-disjointness check workspace including preprocessing | ≤ | $O(nC+n^2+2^{2g})$ | Every family outcome; caller-retained storage is additional. |
| q-ideal-representation-survival | Probability of a passing spread test and low-filtered representation | ≥ | $u^{-100}$ | Low-mean YES regime. |
| q-bounded-representation-success | Success probability of a bounded representation trial | ≥ | $u^{-110}$ | Low-mean YES regime. |
| q-range-sampler-failure | Coupling failure probability for an ordinary range draw | ≤ | $2^{-n^3}$ | Each call, conditional on its preceding history. |
| q-prime-sampler-failure | Coupling failure probability for a prime draw | ≤ | $e^{-n^2/4}$ | Each bounded prime call. |
| q-round-count | Main-branch amplification rounds | = | $u^{120}$ | Each run unless an earlier YES terminates it. |
| q-trial-operation-cap | Underlying word-operation budget per trial | ≤ | $u^{500}2^{\lceil n/2\rceil}$ | Every trial outcome. |
| q-main-branch-total-time | Total main-branch time | ≤ | $The total main-branch time is consequently $$O\bigl(u^{620}2^{\lceil n/2\rceil}+\operatorname{poly}(u)\bigr).$$$ | Every random outcome. |

Entropy is concave, symmetric about $1/2$, and increasing on $[0,1/2]$. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:entropy-numbers}\n h_*<.8114,\\qquad h(1/3)<14/15,\\qquad h(1/6)>5/8.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-138.2"
        }

      "computational_model": "Bounded-width word operations and indexed stored arrays.",
      "effectivity": "effective",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-streams",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A_1,A_2$ be stored constant-word item arrays whose indices have $O(n)$ bits. Let $p\\mid P$ be positive integers, and let $c,v$ be specified integer residues. Assume that the data occupy constantly many words each and that arithmetic, comparisons, and indexed access on them take $O(1)$ word operations in the stated model. The pairs $(a,b)\\in A_1\\times A_2$ whose actual weights sum to $c\\pmod p$ can be streamed in nondecreasing order of either $$(\\omega(a)+\\omega(b))\\bmod P\n \\quad\\text{or}\\quad\n (v-\\omega(a)-\\omega(b))\\bmod P,$$ where all residues are normalized to $\\{0,\\ldots,P-1\\}$. Initialization costs $O(n(|A_1|+|A_2|+1))$ word operations, each subsequent advance costs $O(n)$, and the state together with the input arrays uses $O(|A_1|+|A_2|+1)$ words.*\n\n\n*Consider two such streams of lengths $N_1,N_2$, with combined states and arrays of at most $S$ words. If their equal-key Cartesian products contain $Z$ pairs in all, they can report all these pairs using $O(S+B)$ words and, apart from initialization and the work performed on reported pairs, $$\\label{eq:tie-cost}\n O\\left(B+n(N_1+N_2+Z)+\\frac SB Z\\right)$$ operations. Here $B\\ge1$ is an integer buffer capacity. In particular, if $S\\le\\operatorname{poly}(n)B$, the bound is $\\operatorname{poly}(n)(B+N_1+N_2+Z)$. The same upper bounds hold for an execution stopped partway, with $Z$ still counting *all potential* matches. No coprimality assumption is needed.",
          "source_id": "PM-138.2"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-prefix",
          "exact_quote": "Fix positive integers $C,q$ and a nonnegative integer $m$. On each side $j\\in\\{L,R\\}$, let $\\mathcal T_j$ be a finite collection of raw tuple occurrences. Each occurrence has a bucket in $\\{0,\\ldots,q-1\\}$ and a top mask in $\\{0,1\\}^m$. A valid occurrence produces an entry consisting of its exact weight and top mask. Assume that occurrences producing the same entry have the same bucket. Let $\\mathcal U_j$ be a finite ambient set containing all distinct valid entries, with bucket and mask labels extending those of the generated entries. Write $$J_j=|\\mathcal T_j|,\\qquad U_j=|\\mathcal U_j|.$$ A task for bucket $z$ and prefix $\\sigma$ generates exactly the raw occurrences with these labels, unless it stops early, and reports overflow at the $(C+1)$st distinct valid entry. Suppose a task with $Z$ potential occurrences costs at most $H+KZ$, even if stopped partway, for fixed nonnegative $H,K$.*\n\n\n\n\n*Apply the left-skip and right-prefix schedule of Section <a href=\"#alg:parents\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"alg:parents\">4.3</a> to these data, ignoring early YES answers and the operation cap. Then its number $T$ of parent-generation tasks, its total parent-generation work $W_{\\mathrm{parent}}$, and the total input-array length $N_{\\mathrm{check}}$ of its final checks satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n T&\\le 2q+2(m+1)U_R/C,\\label{eq:prefix-tasks}\\\\\n W_{\\mathrm{parent}}\n &\\le H\\bigl(2q+2(m+1)U_R/C\\bigr)\n       +K\\bigl(J_L+(m+1)J_R\\bigr),\\label{eq:prefix-work}\\\\\n N_{\\mathrm{check}}&\\le U_L+(2m+3)U_R.\\label{eq:prefix-checks}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Every reuse of a left array counts separately in $N_{\\mathrm{check}}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-138.2"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-deficiency-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-deficiency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-deficiency): Bound sumset growth, translate sumsets under sign toggles, and use a replacement-coupled exposure martingale. -> Deficiency monotonicity, superadditivity, sign invariance, replacement sensitivity, and variance control.
- **step-exact-entropy-counting** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy): Compare the modal binomial probability with the total outcome count and certify strict entropy inequalities through integer powers. -> Binomial upper and lower bounds and exact strict entropy constants.
- **step-finite-prime-collisions** [claim](#stmt-lem-prime) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prime): Lower-bound prime abundance with a central binomial coefficient and bound the number of distinct prime factors of a weight difference. -> Prime-range cardinality and divisibility probability bounds.
- **step-sorted-streams-and-replay** [claim](#stmt-lem-streams) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-streams): Sort residue slices, select cyclic pivots, heap-merge rows, and replay large tie suffixes from shared-array snapshots. -> Sorted eligible pair streams and stopped-execution tie-join accounting.
- **step-bounded-sampling** [claim](#stmt-lem-samplers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-samplers): Limit uniform range and prime rejection attempts, and couple each adaptive call to ideal repeat-until-success sampling. -> Uniform conditional call laws, deterministic attempt limits, and aggregate coupling-loss bounds.
- **step-compressed-trial** [claim](#stmt-lem-compressed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compressed): Compact disjoint block sumsets, stream generated groups by residue, cap the left dictionary, and control the fixed solution's bucket by a prime collision estimate. -> One-sided compressed trial with deterministic resource bounds and positive success in the high-mean case.
- **step-parent-record-validity** [claim](#stmt-alg-validity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-validity): Check local shared-mask disjointness and retain exact-weight–top-mask keys before final matching. -> Witness validity, safe deduplication, and sound YES answers.
- **step-prefix-accounting** [claim](#stmt-lem-prefix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prefix): Charge overflow task starts to disjoint ambient entries, raw processing to prefix depth, and repeated left arrays to nonroot tasks. -> Separate task-count, generation-work, and check-input bounds.
- **step-weighted-separation** [claim](#stmt-lem-separator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separator): Sample biased separating families, build mask-incidence lists, traverse family-index intervals depth first, and bound expected record incidences by a sharp entropy inequality. -> Sound weighted-disjointness checking, fixed-pair coverage, deterministic workspace, and expected work.
- **step-independent-family-reuse** [claim](#stmt-cor-separator-reuse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-separator-reuse): Fix the complete family-independent check list, sum expected work, and intersect designated-pair coverage with a Markov work bound. -> Aggregate check-work and coverage guarantee while paying preprocessing once.
- **step-low-filter-survival** [claim](#stmt-lem-representation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-representation): Randomize the solution by sign toggles, balance mixer halves, retain distinct fixed-cardinality representatives, and convert collision bounds into occupied residues. -> A surviving representation fixed for analysis before the extra prime and separating families.
- **step-resources-and-solution-bucket** [claim](#stmt-lem-resources) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-resources): Subtract unconditional resource failure from representation survival, expose the independent extra prime, complete the solution's right prefix, and apply conditional family reuse before subtracting sampling loss. -> Inverse-polynomial success for a representation trial within its deterministic operation cap.
- **step-uniform-program-and-amplification** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-47): Implement exact word arithmetic, install a fixed finite guard and exhaustive fallback, run both capped trials sequentially, account for simultaneous storage, and amplify with fresh rounds. -> Uniform one-sided randomized decision guarantee and simultaneous resource bounds for every execution in each fixed polynomial-bit class.
- **step-ordinary-space-corollary** [claim](#stmt-cor-target) / [proof](#pm-h-47): Absorb the fixed polynomial workspace factor into a strictly larger exponential space rate. -> Ordinary exponential word space, including the stated target rate, with unchanged correctness and time guarantees.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-prefix-task-bound**: Overflow witnesses distinct ambient entries, so prefix refinement bounds task starts by ambient entries and prefix depth.
- **inv-prefix-occurrence-work**: Task overhead is charged per task, while raw occurrence processing is charged by prefix depth, including invalid occurrences and repeated representations.
- **inv-prefix-check-inputs**: Completed right chunks contain disjoint entry sets, and every reuse of a retained left array contributes separately to the final-check input length.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-deduplicate-by-weight**: Merge parent records whenever their actual weights agree. -> Different top masks can have different compatibility with the opposite parent, so merging them can remove a valid final match.
- **anti-late-prefix-filter**: Generate every raw tuple for every prefix task and reject incompatible masks afterward. -> An occurrence can then be processed in incompatible tasks at the same depth, invalidating the depth-based work charge.
- **anti-work-from-dictionary-size**: Estimate generation work solely from the number of retained distinct entries. -> Repeated occurrences and tuples rejected for local overlap can dominate generation even when the dictionary is small.
- **anti-adaptive-family-reuse**: Use the separating families to select which arrays or witness receive the aggregate work guarantee. -> Selection can change the conditional family distribution, removing the premise needed for the coverage and work argument.
- **anti-promote-finite-accounting-test**: Treat finite accounting tests as certification of the full randomized Subset Sum algorithm and its asymptotic constants. -> The tests instantiate the prefix schedule and its cost premise; they do not establish representation survival, separator work, sampling coupling, or the absolute main-branch threshold.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The prefix-accounting lemma specifies a finite dictionary schedule on bucketed raw occurrences and finite ambient entry sets. The executable construction instantiates its stopped-task premise with synthetic work equal to task overhead plus cost per potential occurrence. Coverage is limited to this finite accounting result.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with C,q,m,H,K,L,R,UL,UR; occurrences are [bucket,mask,weight,valid], ambient entries are [bucket,mask,weight].
# PRECONDITIONS: Positive C,q; nonnegative m,H,K; integer weights and valid bucket/mask labels; ambient sets contain generated valid entries with consistent buckets.
# OUTPUTS: JSON tasks,potential,work,check_input for the complete left-skip/right-prefix schedule.
# TIME: Finite traversal; each task scans its eligible raw occurrences and inserts until overflow.
# SPACE: Input collections, one retained left dictionary, one current dictionary, and a depth-first prefix stack.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python integers and exact set membership; no numerical approximations.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an error; full-mask overflow is dropped without a final check.
import json,sys

def load():
    a=json.load(sys.stdin)
    for name in ('C','q','m','H','K'):
        if type(a[name]) is not int: raise ValueError('integer parameter required')
    C,q,m,H,K=(a[x] for x in ('C','q','m','H','K'))
    if C<1 or q<1 or min(m,H,K)<0: raise ValueError('parameter range')
    for side in ('L','R'):
        ambient=set()
        labels={}
        for row in a['U'+side]:
            if len(row)!=3 or any(type(x) is not int for x in row): raise ValueError('ambient entry')
            z,mask,weight=row
            if not 0<=z<q or not 0<=mask<(1<<m): raise ValueError('ambient label')
            key=(weight,mask)
            if key in labels and labels[key]!=z: raise ValueError('inconsistent bucket')
            labels[key]=z
            ambient.add(tuple(row))
        for row in a[side]:
            if len(row)!=4: raise ValueError('occurrence')
            z,mask,weight,valid=row
            if any(type(x) is not int for x in (z,mask,weight)) or type(valid) is not bool: raise ValueError('occurrence types')
            if not 0<=z<q or not 0<=mask<(1<<m): raise ValueError('occurrence label')
            if valid and (z,mask,weight) not in ambient: raise ValueError('missing ambient entry')
    return a

def main():
    a=load()
    C,q,m,H,K=(a[x] for x in ('C','q','m','H','K'))
    tasks=potential=checks=0
    def task(rows,z,depth,prefix):
        nonlocal tasks,potential
        eligible=[]
        for bucket,mask,weight,valid in rows:
            if bucket!=z: continue
            if (mask >> (m-depth)) != prefix: continue
            eligible.append((mask,weight,valid))
        tasks+=1
        potential+=len(eligible)
        entries=set()
        for mask,weight,valid in eligible:
            if not valid: continue
            key=(weight,mask)
            entries.add(key)
            if len(entries)>C: return entries,True
        return entries,False
    for z in range(q):
        left_entries,overflow=task(a['L'],z,0,0)
        if overflow or not left_entries: continue
        stack=[(0,0)]
        while stack:
            depth,prefix=stack.pop()
            entries,overflow=task(a['R'],z,depth,prefix)
            if overflow:
                if depth<m:
                    stack.append((depth+1,2*prefix+1))
                    stack.append((depth+1,2*prefix))
            elif entries:
                checks += len(left_entries) + len(entries)
    print(json.dumps({'tasks':tasks,'potential':potential,'work':H*tasks+K*potential,'check_input':checks},sort_keys=True))
if __name__=='__main__': main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | A Subset Sum decision implementation can execute the compressed-block and representation trials sequentially, enforce their operation caps, reset trial state, and repeat with fresh randomness. Integration must preserve the counted workspace and the possibility of rejecting a solvable instance. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction decides exact subset-weight feasibility. It does not construct a game state transition, collision response, or simulation evolution rule; a connection from such a workload to the stated input problem is ABSENT. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A decision-only feasibility tool can use the compressed-block construction: compact sums on disjoint domains, stream complementary combinations, and retain a bounded residue dictionary. Its output contract supports a YES decision without retaining a solution witness. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The filtered pair-stream construction sorts eligible partner slices, traverses each row in modular key order, and heap-merges rows. Its tie join reports Cartesian matches while buffering occurrences and replaying saved mutable state, avoiding storage of the full product. |
| data_structures | True | strong | implementable | inferred | A bounded binary trie provides deterministic deduplication of exact signed weights and, where required, membership masks. An append-only array exposes distinct retained entries to later searches; overflow bounds simultaneous storage even when a rejected key has introduced transient trie nodes. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The resource analysis assumes a classical word-operation interface and explicitly charges arithmetic that exceeds its width. A circuit, accelerator mapping, parallel execution schedule, or device-memory accounting is ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Random primes here bound collisions between fixed unequal integer weights for modular search filters. The construction supplies neither a cryptographic primitive nor an attack contract against a specified cryptosystem; those applications are ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The deficiency concentration concerns random index domains within an arbitrary fixed input. It does not establish a learned predictor, a training procedure, or a generalization guarantee for numerical data. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The construction can serve as a randomized feasibility subroutine when an operations-research task is exactly the selection of indexed positive integer weights summing to a target. It checks equality feasibility through exact complementary weights and disjoint masks; an objective-minimization or maximization contract is ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The implementation processes trials sequentially with reset state, and the separator traversal retains local parent arrays along a depth-first path. Distributed task ownership, communication, consistency, and failure recovery contracts are ABSENT. |
| other (Bounded exact sampling for combinatorial search) | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The bounded sampler generates finite-range uniform integers by rejection with a fixed attempt limit and reports failure upon exhaustion. Successive range draws construct permutations and fixed-size subsets while maintaining the conditional uniform law needed by the search analysis. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-original-input-domain** (hypothesis): The original decision contract uses positive integer weights and a nonnegative target, with choices identified by indices. Repeated numerical values and the empty subset are allowed. Blocked use: ['Apply the original-input guarantee directly to arbitrary signed or noninteger weights.', 'Collapse repeated input values into a single selectable index.']
- **limit-probabilistic-completeness** (formal_scope): YES answers are sound, but a NO answer can reject a solvable instance. The randomized guarantee does not provide deterministic completeness on every execution. Blocked use: ['Use a returned NO as an exact infeasibility certificate.', 'Require every execution to accept every solvable instance.']
- **limit-fixed-polynomial-bit-regime** (hypothesis): The asymptotic resource guarantee is assessed separately within each fixed polynomial-bit class. Its constants and threshold may depend on the class, while correctness applies to every individual input. Blocked use: ['Claim the same asymptotic constants for unrestricted input bit lengths.', 'Infer the main resource bound for an individual input without establishing its regime and threshold.']
- **limit-charged-word-model** (formal_scope): The resource bounds count bounded-width word operations and all writable state. Wider arithmetic, preprocessing, sampling, addresses, and retained randomness cannot be treated as free. Blocked use: ['Translate the operation bound directly into elapsed time on an unspecified platform.', 'Use an uncounted random tape, external table, or wider unit-cost arithmetic.']
- **limit-space-endpoint-prefactor** (formal_scope): Ordinary space absorption requires a fixed exponent strictly above the endpoint. The polynomial workspace factor remains at the endpoint. Blocked use: ['Remove the polynomial factor while retaining the endpoint space exponent.', 'Treat polynomial-suppressed and ordinary space as interchangeable.']
- **limit-main-branch-threshold** (unknown): The main construction requires its guard and a sufficiently large absolute threshold. A concrete threshold and operational implementation constants are ABSENT from the supplied paper. Blocked use: ['Choose an arbitrary practical threshold and claim the asymptotic trial proofs already apply.', 'Omit exact enumeration for inputs outside the guarded branch.']
- **limit-decision-without-witness** (formal_scope): The algorithm's required output is a decision. Sound acceptance relies on the existence of subset witnesses, but the compressed trial need not retain or return those witnesses. Blocked use: ['Promise a selected subset from the stated decision procedure.', 'Assume witness extraction inherits the stated storage bound without additional accounting.']
- **limit-deficiency-is-analysis-only** (formal_scope): Mean deficiency selects the applicable success proof rather than an executable branch. The algorithm runs both trials with fresh randomness. Blocked use: ['Replace the uniform schedule with a deficiency classifier that the paper does not implement.', 'Apply the success bound of one trial outside its corresponding deficiency regime.']
- **limit-compression-disjoint-domains** (hypothesis): Weight-only compaction is sound for the compression construction because its group domains are disjoint. It cannot replace indexed compatibility checks in the representation construction. Blocked use: ["Reuse compression's weight-only dictionaries for parents sharing indices.", 'Combine exact complementary weights without checking shared-index compatibility.']
- **limit-layout-feasibility-and-spread** (hypothesis): The representation trial requires a passing spread test and feasible integer allocations and prescribed cardinalities. Its construction permits failure when feasibility tests fail. Blocked use: ['Continue a representation trial after a failed spread test.', 'Perform unsigned allocation arithmetic before establishing feasibility.']
- **limit-parent-key-and-local-validity** (hypothesis): Parent records must retain both exact weight and top mask, and local joins must reject overlapping shared masks. Equal weights alone do not determine compatibility. Blocked use: ['Deduplicate parent records by weight alone.', 'Retain a parent tuple as a valid subset before checking local disjointness.']
- **limit-prefix-filter-before-generation** (hypothesis): The prefix accounting requires incompatible leaf items to be removed before stream generation. Late rejection of incompatible tuples does not justify the depth-based processing charge. Blocked use: ['Generate unrestricted tuples in every prefix task and filter them afterward.', 'Multiply raw tuple processing by all task starts while claiming the stated accounting result.']
- **limit-raw-occurrences-versus-entries** (formal_scope): Generation work depends on raw occurrences, including repetitions and invalid local overlaps. Distinct dictionary size controls different parts of the accounting and cannot replace the raw count. Blocked use: ['Estimate parent generation solely from retained dictionary size.', 'Discard repeated occurrences or invalid tuples from generation-work accounting.']
- **limit-stopped-stream-potential-matches** (hypothesis): Stopped tie joins are bounded using all potential matches, including matches not reached. Snapshot copying, replay, initialization, and reported-pair processing require their respective charges. Blocked use: ['Charge a stopped join only to pairs already reported.', 'Treat copied mutable generator state as free or duplicate immutable arrays unnecessarily.']
- **limit-extra-prime-equal-weights** (edge_case): The prime collision bound applies to fixed nonzero weight differences. Equal-weight records remain together and require mask-cardinality control on the left and prefix refinement on the right. Blocked use: ['Apply the unequal-weight collision estimate to equal-weight records.', 'Assume a random prime alone makes every parent bucket fit.']
- **limit-separator-fixed-pair-and-expected-work** (formal_scope): Separator coverage concerns a fixed valid pair under the specified independent family law. The work bound after preprocessing is an expectation with an exponential incidence factor, whereas workspace is bounded on every outcome. Blocked use: ['Claim simultaneous coverage of every possible pair from the fixed-pair guarantee.', 'Claim polynomial work per record or deterministic completion time from the expected-work statement.', 'Omit family preprocessing or caller-retained arrays from resource accounting.']
- **limit-family-reuse-conditional-law** (hypothesis): Reusing separating families requires their independent conditional distributions to remain unchanged after fixing the complete check list, its parameters, and any designated pair. Delayed exposure alone does not establish that condition. Blocked use: ['Select checks or a designated witness using the separating families and then invoke the reuse guarantee.', 'Infer independence merely because the families have not yet been inspected.']
- **limit-rare-survival-resource-conditioning** (hypothesis): Resource failure is bounded jointly and unconditionally before its probability is subtracted from rare representation survival. The proof does not condition the resource estimate on successful survival. Blocked use: ['Reuse an unconditional resource probability as though it were unchanged under rare survival conditioning.', 'Expose the extra prime or families before fixing the representation required by the proof.']
- **limit-bounded-sampling-exhaustion** (edge_case): A bounded sampler can exhaust its attempts and fail the trial. Uniformity conditional on an individual call accepting does not justify conditioning the entire adaptive experiment on every sampler succeeding. Blocked use: ['Replace exhausted sampling with a default accepted value.', 'Use unbounded retries while claiming deterministic trial budgets.', 'Condition all adaptive choices jointly on successful sampling without accounting for bias.']
- **limit-cap-and-reset-discipline** (hypothesis): The finite execution guarantee depends on trial operation caps, sampling-failure propagation, fresh round randomness, and reset storage. Favorable resource events establish success rather than permission to exceed those caps. Blocked use: ['Continue a trial beyond its budget because a representation might survive.', 'Accumulate dictionaries, replay state, or discarded randomness across tasks or rounds.', 'Treat a favorable-event work estimate as the resource guarantee for every execution.']
- **limit-finite-accounting-certification-scope** (formal_scope): A finite executable check of prefix accounting can validate supplied schedules and synthetic task charges. It does not establish the complete randomized algorithm's representation survival, sampling law, separator estimates, or asymptotic threshold. Blocked use: ['Promote agreement on finite prefix schedules into certification of the full resource theorem.', 'Interpret synthetic task work as measured implementation cost.']
- **limit-operational-evidence-absent** (unknown): Measured runtime, measured memory use, and target-platform deployment evidence are ABSENT from the supplied paper. Asymptotic word-model guarantees alone do not establish practical operational fit. Blocked use: ['Assert measured performance or deployability from the theorem alone.', 'Promise an interactive execution budget without an implementation and platform assessment.']

      "eng_class": "ALGO",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "quote": "There is a single uniform classical randomized algorithm in this model that never answers YES incorrectly and answers each YES instance correctly with probability at least $2/3$. For every fixed positive integer $c$, there are a constant $A_c>0$ and positive integers $k_c,N_c$ such that, whenever $n\\ge N_c$ and $b\\le n^c$, every outcome of the algorithm’s randomness uses at most $$A_c n^{k_c}2^{n/2}\\quad\\text{operations}\n \\qquad\\text{and}\\qquad\n A_c(n+b+2)^{15}2^{0.199n}\\quad\\text{writable words}.$$ The algorithm does not depend on $c$.",
          "quote_sha256": "89ad9d33e2764a2836fc40f469a64e1b5d4013c698abf657e79e14205aa9e95a"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "sec:implementation",
          "quote": "The full schedule is as follows. On either exceptional case in the guard, enumerate all indexed subsets exactly. Otherwise perform $u^{120}$ rounds. In each round run the compressed-block trial and then the representation trial, sequentially, using fresh randomness and reset trial state. Abort each trial after $u^{500}2^{\\lceil n/2\\rceil}$ underlying word operations; a sampling failure has the same effect as an abort. A YES from either trial stops the program. If no trial returns YES, return NO after the last round.",
          "quote_sha256": "0374680284f6e15e87ea11d814555b03828a4be5e098e1a0ffefa8b36b7539b7"
        }
      ],
      "hypotheses": "Use the stated uniform word model, count all retained state and sampling work, and enforce the guarded trial schedule.",
      "input": "Positive integer array and nonnegative target.",
      "maturity": "adaptable",
      "output": "YES only for a solvable instance; NO may be a false negative under the stated probability guarantee.",
      "task": "Build a sound randomized Subset Sum decision component with explicit resource accounting.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },

      "basis": "stated",

          "quote_sha256": "b1277b582e38e124e14150c2dd20fd8d0a3f65946a1e979886e4c020d24d0251"
        }

      "maturity": "implementable",

      "task": "Deduplicate bounded weighted records without losing shared-mask compatibility.",

          "quote": "Every raw four-leaf tuple has a well-defined top mask because $T_1,\\ldots,T_4$ partition $M_0$ and each occurs in exactly one leaf on each side.       "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-separator",
          "quote": "For the input and independent families just specified, there is a weighted-disjointness check with the following guarantees, for all sufficiently large $n$.*\n\n1.  *It returns YES only for a pair satisfying <a href=\"#sep:target\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sep:target\">[sep:target]</a>. For every fixed pair satisfying that relation, the probability that the check finds a solution is at least $1-4e^{-10}$.*\n\n2.  *Preprocessing the families takes at most $\\operatorname{poly}(n)2^{2g}$ operations and words. Thereafter the expected check work is at most $$(|\\mathcal L|+|\\mathcal R|)\\,u^{22}2^{(1-h_*)m}.$$*\n\n3.  *Workspace, including preprocessing, is deterministically at most $O(nC+n^2+2^{2g})$ words. Any execution stopped early has no larger work or space usage than the complete traversal used in these bounds.*\n\n*The probability statements concern the stated ideal uniform families. Generating them with at most $n^3$ attempts per ordinary rejection draw also has deterministic cost $\\operatorname{poly}(n)2^{2g}$, or reports sampling failure. It can be coupled to the ideal families with failure probability $\\exp(-\\Omega(n^2))$.",
          "quote_sha256": "1d67f0a6ee6f107588a4c6fe15190e45dc4c901833adc882a90d8191beb7dc51"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-32",
          "quote": "Process the blocks in order. For the current block, begin with its whole index interval $[1,\\nu_i]$. Filter both received arrays to records having a compatible index in this interval. If either filtered array is empty, finish this branch. Otherwise split a nonsingleton interval into two nearly equal intervals and visit both children. At a singleton, fix that family index and enter the next block with the filtered arrays. After fixing all four indices, sort the two arrays by actual weight and search for weights summing exactly to $t'$.",
          "quote_sha256": "97adcc9d4c94dce7e671a4fce2bc4daeeffd6e8722c5cc85e39337948fd7cdb6"
        }
      ],
      "hypotheses": "Families have the specified independent uniform distributions and are independent of the arrays; preprocessing and caller storage are charged separately.",
      "input": "Bounded parent arrays satisfying the balanced per-block mask cardinalities and an exact target.",
      "maturity": "adaptable",
      "output": "A sound YES decision when a separated complementary pair is found.",
      "task": "Check complementary weights with disjoint masks using sampled separating families.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },

      "contract_anchor": "stmt-lem-samplers",
      "eng_class": "PROB-SIM",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-samplers",
          "quote": "A uniform integer in $\\{0,\\ldots,D-1\\}$, with $1\\le D<2^w$, can be sampled using at most $n^3$ rejection attempts, or the trial can report failure. Conditional on any preceding history and on this call accepting, its result is uniform in the specified range. It couples to an ideal draw with failure probability at most $2^{-n^3}$.*\n\n*For $4\\le\\ell\\le n$, the same attempt limit gives a uniform prime in $\\mathcal P_\\ell$, conditional on this call accepting, and coupling failure at most $e^{-n^2/4}$. A full trial, including permutations, signs, residues and the four separating families, couples to the ideal experiment with total failure $\\exp(-\\Omega(n^2))$. All constants are absolute. All permitted attempts are included in the deterministic work bounds.",
          "quote_sha256": "63567ea44914449cbf84b2c6f1a8562eb51aade5e896187f85c41d92ac81e38a"
        }
      ],
      "hypotheses": "Range widths and prime parameters satisfy the sampler lemma; retain reused randomness only in counted storage.",
      "input": "A finite range determined by the current execution history and fresh random words.",
      "maturity": "implementable",
      "output": "A uniform accepted value or trial failure.",
      "task": "Replace ideal adaptive range draws with bounded uniform samplers.",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    }

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give a uniform classical randomized decision algorithm for worst-case Subset Sum. Under every fixed polynomial bound on input-integer bit length, it uses $`\mathop{\mathrm{poly}}\nolimits (n)2^{n/2}`$ time and ordinary $`O(2^{n/5})`$ writable words of $`O(n+b)`$ bits, where <i>b</i> is the largest input bit length. Both resource bounds hold on every execution. The error is one-sided: the algorithm always rejects unsolvable instances and accepts each solvable instance with probability at least 2/3.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
